You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and i+1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters of water
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's define:
- Let R_i be the rain received by Mountain i (this is always even)
- When Mountain i receives 2x liters, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters

So for each dam i:
- Dam i gets water from Mountain i (via R_i/2) and from Mountain i+1 (via R_{i+1}/2)
- Therefore: A_i = R_i/2 + R_{i+1}/2

This gives us: R_i + R_{i+1} = 2A_i

- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
- R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3
- ...

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate R_1 using the alternating sum formula
R = [0] * N
R[0] = 0
for i in range(N):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even index (0-indexed), which corresponds to odd position (1-indexed)
        R[0] += A[i]
    else:  # odd index (0-indexed), which corresponds to even position (1-indexed)
        R[0] -= A[i]

# Build the rest of R using recurrence
for i in range(1, N):
    R[i] = 2 * A[i-1] - R[i-1]

print(' '.join(map(str, R)))

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1, with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulate x liters of water
4. We're given the total water accumulated in each dam (A_i), and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's define:
- Let R_i be the rain amount for Mountain i (which is even)
- When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, it contributes x liters to Dam i-1 and x liters to Dam i

So:
- Dam 1 accumulates: (rain from Mountain 1)/2 + (rain from Mountain 2)/2 = R₁/2 + R₂/2
- Dam 2 accumulates: (rain from Mountain 2)/2 + (rain from Mountain 3)/2 = R₂/2 + R₃/2
- Dam i accumulates: R_{i-1}/2 + R_i/2

Given that Dam i has A_i liters, we have:
- A₁ = R₁/2 + R₂/2
- A₂ = R₂/2 + R₃/2
- ...
- A_N = R_N/2 + R₁/2

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's multiply each equation by 2:
- 2A₁ = R₁ + R₂
- 2A₂ = R₂ + R₃
- 2A₃ = R₃ + R₄
- ...
- 2A_N = R_N + R₁

This gives us a system of equations. Since N is odd, we can solve this systematically.

Let me try to express R₂ in terms of R₁ from the first equation:
- R₂ = 2A₁ - R₁

Substitute into the second equation:
- 2A₂ = R₂ + R₃ = (2A₁ - R₁) + R₃
- R₃ = 2A₂ - (2A₁ - R₁) = 2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁

Continue this pattern:
- R₄ = 2A₃ - R₃ = 2A₃ - (2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁) = 2A₃ - 2A₂ + 2A₁ - R₁

I notice a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, when we get to the last equation, we can substitute and solve for R₁.

Let me think of it differently. If I sum all equations:
- Σ(2A_i) = Σ(R_i) + Σ(R_i) = 2Σ(R_i)
- So Σ(R_i) = Σ(A_i)

That means the total rain is the sum of all A_i.

Now, let me work through the equations:
- R₂ = 2A₁ - R₁
- R₃ = 2A₂ - R₂ = 2A₂ - (2A₁ - R₁) = 2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁
- R₄ = 2A₃ - R₃ = 2A₃ - (2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁) = 2A₃ - 2A₂ + 2A₁ - R₁
- R₅ = 2A₄ - R₄ = 2A₄ - (2A₃ - 2A₂ + 2A₁ - R₁) = 2A₄ - 2A₃ + 2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁

I can see that the pattern alternates: +R₁, -R₁, +R₁, -R₁...

Since N is odd, when I reach R_N, the coefficient of R₁ will be (-1)^(N-1) = 1 (since N-1 is even).

For the last equation (N-th):
- 2A_N = R_N + R₁

Substituting the pattern:
- R_N = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}
- And so on...

Actually, let me implement the direct calculation:

For i from 1 to N-1:
- R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i

And for the last equation:
- 2A_N = R_N + R₁

Since N is odd, we can find R₁ by:
- R_{N+1} = 2A_N - R_N (but R_{N+1} = R₁)

So: R₁ = 2A_N - R_N

But we can compute R_N from the chain:
- R₂ = 2A₁ - R₁
- R₃ = 2A₂ - R₂ = 2A₂ - (2A₁ - R₁) = 2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁
- R₄ = 2A₃ - R₃ = 2A₃ - (2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁) = 2A₃ - 2A₂ + 2A₁ - R₁

The pattern is: R_k has coefficient (-1)^(k-1) for R₁.

So for R_N, the coefficient of R₁ is (-1)^(N-1).

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1.

Therefore: R_N = (some expression involving A_i) + R₁

Actually, let me make a simpler observation:

From the system:
- R₂ = 2A₁ - R₁
- R₃ = 2A₂ - R₂ = 2A₂ - 2A₁ + R₁
- R₄ = 2A₃ - R₃ = 2A₃ - 2A₂ + 2A₁ - R₁
- ...

So the pattern is that for position k, R_k = (-1)^(k-1) * R₁ + f(k)

For the last equation: 2A_N = R_N + R₁
So: R₁ = 2A_N - R_N

Now I can compute all R_i from R₁ using the recurrence.

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# We know R1 = 2*A[N-1] - R_N
# We can compute all R_i in terms of R1 using the recurrence
# R[i+1] = 2*A[i] - R[i]

# Let's compute all rain amounts
R = [0] * N

# Start with R0 (which is R1)
# We need to find R1 first

# For an odd N, we have the relation:
# R1 = 2*A[N-1] - R_N
# And R_N can be expressed as a linear combination of R1 and A values

# Using the recurrence: R[i] = 2*A[i-1] - R[i-1] (for i from 1 to N-1)
# Actually, index: R[i] = 2*A[i] - R[i-1] for i from 0 to N-2
# Then R[N-1] = 2*A[N-2] - R[N-2]

# We can compute everything in terms of R0
# R1 = R0
# R2 = 2*A[0] - R1 = 2*A[0] - R0  
# R3 = 2*A[1] - R2 = 2*A[1] - (2*A[0] - R0) = 2*A[1] - 2*A[0] + R0
# R4 = 2*A[2] - R3 = 2*A[2] - (2*A[1] - 2*A[0] + R0) = 2*A[2] - 2*A[1] + 2*A[0] - R0

# The pattern: R_k = (-1)^(k-1) * R0 + sum_{j=0}^{k-2} (-1)^(k-2-j) * 2*A[j]

# For R_N (index N-1), k = N, so R[N-1] = (-1)^(N-1) * R0 + sum of terms

# Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1
# So R[N-1] = R0 + sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^(N-2-j) * 2*A[j]

# The last equation is 2*A[N-1] = R[N-1] + R[0]
# So: 2*A[N-1] = R0 + sum + R0 = 2*R0 + sum
# Therefore: 2*R0 = 2*A[N-1] - sum
# So: R0 = A[N-1] - (sum / 2)

# But wait, let me recalculate the sum carefully

# R[k] = (-1)^(k-1) * R0 + sum from j=0 to k-2 of (-1)^(k-2-j) * 2*A[j]

# For k = N-1: R[N-1] = (-1)^(N-2) * R0 + sum from j=0 to N-3 of (-1)^(N-3-j) * 2*A[j]

# Since N is odd, N-2 is odd, so (-1)^(N-2) = -1
# So R[N-1] = -R0 + sum

# Last equation: 2*A[N-1] = R[N-1] + R0 = -R0 + sum + R0 = sum
# So sum = 2*A[N-1]

# This suggests my pattern might be wrong.

# Let me try brute force the small case:
# N=3, A=[2,2,4]
# We want R=[4,0,4]

# Equations:
# 2A1 = R1 + R2 → 4 = R1 + R2
# 2A2 = R2 + R3 → 4 = R2 + R3  
# 2A3 = R3 + R1 → 8 = R3 + R1

# From first: R2 = 4 - R1
# From second: R3 = 4 - R2 = 4 - (4 - R1) = R1
# From third: 8 = R3 + R1 = R1 + R1 = 2R1 → R1 = 4
# Then R2 = 0, R3 = 4

# So the pattern: R2 = 2A1 - R1, R3 = 2A2 - R2, and finally 2A3 = R3 + R1

# Algorithm:
# 1. Set R[0] = R1
# 2. For i from 0 to N-2: R[i+1] = 2*A[i] - R[i]
# 3. Then check: 2*A[N-1] = R[N-1] + R[0]
# 4. But we already know the solution is unique.

# Since we have the final constraint, we can solve for R[0] from the last equation.

# From the recurrence, R[N-1] can be expressed as a function of R[0] and the A's.
# And we have 2*A[N-1] = R[N-1] + R[0]

# So R[0] = 2*A[N-1] - R[N-1]

# But R[N-1] can be computed from R[0] using the recurrence.

# Instead, I'll implement the recurrence and solve for R[0].

# Actually, let me solve the system directly.

# Let me denote the recurrence: R[i+1] = 2*A[i] - R[i]
# With R[0] = x (unknown)

# Then:
# R[1] = 2*A[0] - x
# R[2] = 2*A[1] - R[1] = 2*A[1] - (2*A[0] - x) = 2*A[1] - 2*A[0] + x
# R[3] = 2*A[2] - R[2] = 2*A[2] - (2*A[1] - 2*A[0] + x) = 2*A[2] - 2*A[1] + 2*A[0] - x

# The pattern is: R[k] = (-1)^k * x + sum of alternating terms

# So for R[N-1]: R[N-1] = (-1)^(N-1) * x + sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^{N-2-j} * 2*A[j]

# Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1
# Therefore: R[N-1] = x + sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^{N-2-j} * 2*A[j]

# The last equation is: 2*A[N-1] = R[N-1] + R[0] = x + sum + x = 2x + sum

# So: 2x = 2*A[N-1] - sum
# x = A[N-1] - (sum / 2)

# The sum is sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^{N-2-j} * 2*A[j]

# But let me verify with the example:
# N=3, A=[2,2,4]
# sum = (-1)^(3-2-0)*2*A[0] + (-1)^(3-2-1)*2*A[1] = (-1)^1*4 + (-1)^0*4 = -4 + 4 = 0
# x = A[2] - (sum/2) = 4 - 0 = 4
# This matches!

# So the algorithm is:
# 1. Compute S = sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^(N-2-j) * 2*A[j]
# 2. x = A[N-1] - S/2
# 3. R[0] = x
# 4. For i from 0 to N-2: R[i+1] = 2*A[i] - R[i]

# But since N is odd, N-2-j ranges from N-2 (even) to 0 (even) when N-2 is even, and the parity alternates.

# Actually, (-1)^(N-2-j) = (-1)^(odd - 2 - j) = (-1)^(odd - j) = (-1)^(odd) * (-1)^(-j) = -1 * (-1)^j = -(-1)^j

# Since N is odd, N-2 is odd.
# So (-1)^(N-2-j) = (-1)^(odd - j) = (-1)^(odd) * (-1)^(-j) = -1 * 1 = -1 when j is even, +1 when j is odd?

# Let me calculate: (-1)^(odd - j) = (-1)^(odd) * (-1)^(-j) = (-1) * (-1)^j = -(-1)^j

# So it's -(-1)^j

# Therefore: S = sum_{j=0}^{N-2} -(-1)^j * 2*A[j] = -2 * sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^j * A[j]

# So x = A[N-1] - S/2 = A[N-1] - (-2 * sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^j * A[j])/2 = A[N-1] + sum_{j=0}^{N-2} (-1)^j * A[j]

# Let me verify with example:
# A=[2,2,4], N=3
# sum_{j=0}^{1} (-1)^j * A[j] = (-1)^0*2 + (-1)^1*2 = 2 - 2 = 0
# x = 4 + 0 = 4 ✓

# Perfect!

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate the sum needed for R[0]
sum_alternating = 0
for j in range(N - 1):  # j from 0 to N-2
    if j % 2 == 0:
        sum_alternating += A[j]
    else:
        sum_alternating -= A[j]

# R[0] = A[N-1] + sum_alternating
R0 = A[N-1] + sum_alternating

# Now compute all rain amounts
rain = [0] * N
rain[0] = R0

for i in range(N - 1):
    rain[i + 1] = 2 * A[i] - rain[i]

print(*rain)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and i+1, with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters (Dam 0 = Dam N)
4. We're given the final water amounts in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say Mountain i receives R_i liters of rain. Since rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i liters of rain:
- Dam i-1 gets x_i liters (from Mountain i)
- Dam i gets x_i liters (from Mountain i)

So for each dam:
- Dam 1: gets (R_1/2) from Mountain 1 + (R_2/2) from Mountain 2 = A_1
- Dam 2: gets (R_2/2) from Mountain 2 + (R_3/2) from Mountain 3 = A_2
- ...
- Dam N: gets (R_N/2) from Mountain N + (R_1/2) from Mountain 1 = A_N

This gives us the system of equations:
- R_1/2 + R_2/2 = A_1
- R_2/2 + R_3/2 = A_2  
- ...
- R_N/2 + R_1/2 = A_N

Multiplying each equation by 2:
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

## Solving the System

We have a circular system:
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
- R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

Let me express each R_i in terms of R_1:
- From equation 1: R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- From equation 2: R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- From equation 3: R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

I notice a pattern with alternating signs. Since N is odd, when I go through all equations and come back to R_1, I should get a consistent solution.

- R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

If I add all equations together:
(R_1 + R_2) + (R_2 + R_3) + (R_3 + R_4) + ... + (R_N + R_1) = 2(A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)

Left side: R_1 + 2R_2 + 2R_3 + ... + 2R_{N-1} + R_N = 2(ΣA_i)

This sums to: R_1 + R_N + 2(Σ_{i=2}^{N-1} R_i) = 2(ΣA_i)

- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

I see the alternating pattern: R_i = (alternating sum of A's) + (-1)^(i-1)*R_1

alternating sum + 2R_1 = 2A_N
2R_1 = 2A_N - alternating sum

c_N + 2x = 2A_N

From the equations:

2x = 2A_N - sum of 2*A_j with alternating signs

The alternating sum is: A_N - A_{N-1} + A_{N-2} - A_{N-3} + ... + A_2 - A_1 (since N is odd, we end with -A_1)

So R_1 = A_N - (A_N - A_{N-1} + A_{N-2} - ... - A_1)

From the system:

From R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1, we get R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
From R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2, we get R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
From R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3, we get R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
And so on...

The pattern is clear: R_i alternates with sign of R_1.

Since N is odd, we can write R_N = some alternating sum of A values + R_1

For R_N: when N is odd, R_N = (2A_{N-1} - 2A_{N-2} + 2A_{N-3} - ... + 2A_2 - 2A_1) + R_1

Wait, looking at the pattern:
- R_1 = R_1
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

So for odd i: R_i = (some sum) + R_1
For even i: R_i = (some sum) - R_1

For R_N where N is odd: R_N = (2(A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_2 - A_1)) + R_1

From R_N + R_1 = 2A_N:
(2(A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_2 - A_1) + R_1) + R_1 = 2A_N
2(A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_2 - A_1) + 2R_1 = 2A_N
R_1 = A_N - (A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_2 - A_1)

Let me define S = A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_2 - A_1
Then R_1 = A_N - S

Then I can compute all other R_i using the recurrence.

Actually, let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]

S = A_2 - A_1 = 2 - 2 = 0
R_1 = A_3 - S = 4 - 0 = 4

R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1 = 2*2 - 4 = 0
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2*2 - 0 = 4

So R = [4,0,4] which matches the expected output!

Perfect! The formula works.

## Algorithm

1. Read N and array A
2. Calculate S = sum of A[i] with alternating signs (from A[N-2] down to A[0], starting with positive)
   - For N=3: S = A[1] - A[0]
3. R[0] = A[N-1] - S
4. Use the recurrence to compute all other R[i]:
   - R[1] = 2*A[0] - R[0]
   - R[2] = 2*A[1] - R[1]
   - ...
   - R[N-1] = 2*A[N-2] - R[N-2]

Wait, let me check the indexing.

From the equations:
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1 → R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2 → R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N → R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

So the recurrence is R[i] = 2*A[i-1] - R[i-1] for i from 1 to N-1, and R[0] = A[N-1] - S.

But for R_N, we already have the direct formula.

Actually, let me define it properly:

R[0] = A[N-1] - S
For i from 1 to N-1:
R[i] = 2*A[i-1] - R[i-1]

Wait, this would give:
R[1] = 2*A[0] - R[0]
R[2] = 2*A[1] - R[1]
...

But we need to verify the last equation.

For the last equation: R[N-1] + R[0] should equal 2*A[N-1].

With our calculation:
R[N-1] = 2*A[N-2] - R[N-2]

We need R[N-1] + R[0] = 2*A[N-1]

Let me trace through the derivation:
We have R_1 = A_N - S where S = A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + ... - A_1
This came from: R_N + R_1 = 2A_N
And R_N = (some alternating sum) + R_1

So the formula is correct.

## Implementation

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate S = A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_1 (since N is odd, ends with A_1 with negative sign)
# More precisely: S = A[N-2] - A[N-3] + A[N-4] - ... - A[0]
S = 0
for i in range(N-2, 0, -1):  # from N-2 down to 1
    if (N-2 - i) % 2 == 0:  # even position in the alternating sum (0-indexed from N-2)
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

# Actually, let me recalculate S correctly:
S = 0
for i in range(N-2, 0, -1):
    if (N-2 - i) % 2 == 0:  # even offset from start (N-2)
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

# Actually, simpler: S = A[N-2] - A[N-3] + A[N-4] - ... + A[1] - A[0]
S = 0
for i in range(N-2, 0, -1):
    if (N-2 - i) % 2 == 0:
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

# For N=3: A[1] - A[0]
# For N=5: A[3] - A[2] + A[1] - A[0]

# Correct S calculation:
S = 0
for i in range(N-2, 0, -1):
    if (N-2 - i) % 2 == 0:
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

# But actually, since we're going from N-2 down to 1, the sign alternates:
# A[N-2] (positive), A[N-3] (negative), A[N-4] (positive), etc.

# Simpler: just iterate from N-2 down to 1 with alternating signs
S = 0
sign = 1
for i in range(N-2, 0, -1):
    S += sign * A[i]
    sign *= -1

R = [0] * N
R[0] = A[N-1] - S

# Now compute R[1] to R[N-1] using recurrence
for i in range(1, N):
    R[i] = 2 * A[i-1] - R[i-1]

print(*R)
```

Wait, let me verify the sign calculation with N=3:
- N-2 = 1, so i goes from 1 down to 1 (just i=1)
- sign starts as 1
- S += 1 * A[1] = 2
- Then R[0] = A[2] - 2 = 4 - 2 = 2, but expected is 4!

I think I messed up. Let me go back to the derivation.

For N=3:
S = A_2 - A_1 = A[1] - A[0] = 2 - 2 = 0
R[0] = A[2] - S = 4 - 0 = 4 ✓

For N=5:
S = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 = A[3] - A[2] + A[1] - A[0]

So the alternating sum goes from A[N-2] down to A[1], with positive, negative, positive, negative...

My sign calculation is wrong.

Actually, in the derivation for R_N:
R_N = (2A_{N-1} - 2A_{N-2} + 2A_{N-3} - ... + 2A_2 - 2A_1) + R_1

So the coefficient of A_j in S is 2*(-1)^(j-1) where j goes from N-1 down to 1.

For the alternating sum part: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_1 (with alternating signs starting with positive for A_{N-1})

Actually, it's: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... - A_1

So from i=N-1 down to i=1:
S = A[N-1] - A[N-2] + A[N-3] - A[N-4] + ... + A[1] - A[0] (if N>3)

Wait, in my N=3 example, if I use A[N-1] = A[2], then S = A[2] - A[1] + A[0]? That would be 4-2+2=4, then R[0]=4-4=0, which is wrong.

Let me re-derive.

For N=3: 
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2
But R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
So R_3 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

From R_3 + R_1 = 2A_3:
(2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) + R_1 = 2A_3
2A_2 - 2A_1 + 2R_1 = 2A_3
R_1 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1)

So S = A_2 - A_1 = A[1] - A[0]

So the alternating sum is from A[N-2] down to A[1] (inclusive), with alternating signs starting with positive.

For general N: S = A[N-2] - A[N-3] + A[N-4] - ... + A[1] - A[0] (if N>3)

Wait for N=3: A[1] - A[0] ✓
For N=5: A[3] - A[2] + A[1] - A[0]

Yes!

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate S = A[N-2] - A[N-3] + A[N-4] - ... + A[1] - A[0]
# This is the alternating sum from A[N-2] down to A[0], starting with positive
S = 0
sign = 1
for i in range(N-2, -1, -1):  # from N-2 down to 0
    S += sign * A[i]
    sign *= -1

# R[0] = A[N-1] - S
R = [0] * N
R[0] = A[N-1] - S

# Compute the rest using recurrence: R[i] = 2*A[i-1] - R[i-1]
for i in range(1, N):
    R[i] = 2 * A[i-1] - R[i-1]

print(*R)
```
